, which we cannot have by total ordering of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
events by
Fig.

3
or by virtual scan
total order by
Fig.

7
. Thus
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}

cannot be equal.

Assuming instead we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}\sqsubset e}
, we derive a
contradiction. By
Lemma

3.1
from
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\prec^{+}e^{\prime}}

we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\vartriangleleft e^{\prime}_{r}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e^{\prime}_{r}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}
. By
Eq.

RB.2
we can derive

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e^{\prime}_{r}\sqsubset e_{r}}
from
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}\sqsubset e}
, we can construct the
cycle
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\vartriangleleft e^{\prime}_{r}\sqsubset e_{r}}
which contradicts irreflexivity
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\vartriangleleft}
(
Lemma

2.1
).
∎

By
Lemma

2.1
and Virtual
Eq.

V.1
, we have that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft}
is irreflexive, and thus it is a strict partial order. We
will use this to prove following properties.

Fig.

5
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
then we must have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}=w^{\prime}_{i}}
.

Proof.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
, by unfolding
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}

we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
, and dually for

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
. By case splitting

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
.
Remember that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
splits into either

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, or

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and similarly for

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
splits into different cases, e.g.,
we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, then by

Fig.

7
we derive a contradiction.

•

If both split into the first case, i.e., we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
, then by

Fig.

3
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}={w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
.
Since each abstract write has a distinct representative write, we
must have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}=w^{\prime}_{i}}
.

•

If both split into the second case, i.e., we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
then by

Fig.

7
we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}=w^{\prime}_{i}}
. ∎

Lemma B.1.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft s}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}
.

Proof.

By viewing
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft s}
as a chain of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft_{1}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft_{1}\dots\vartriangleleft_{1}s}
. By induction on the length of the
chain. In our base case we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft_{1}s}
, which splits
into cases
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\sqsubset s}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\prec s}
, which can only
be
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
. For
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\sqsubset s}
, by

Eqs.

RB.2
and
7
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}
. For
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}
directly from the definition of

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}}
. The inductive step is similar, with the exception
that we need to also consider for

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft_{1}\dots\vartriangleleft_{1}s^{\prime}\vartriangleleft_{1}s}
the
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{sc}}$}}}$}}}s}

case, which by the definition of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{sc}}$}}}$}}}}
gives us

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s^{\prime}}^{\bullet}}\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}
, which implies

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s^{\prime}}^{\bullet}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}
, allowing us to construct

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}
.
∎

Fig.

5
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
then there does not exist a
write
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft s}
holds.

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft_{1}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{wr}$}}$}}}$}}}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\vartriangleleft_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}\trianglelefteqslant\vartriangleleft_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}\trianglelefteqslant\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}

Figure 12
.
Directed acyclic graph
representing the proof of splitting
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
into
four final (boxed) cases. Forks in the graph with
\to
arrows
represents case splitting, splitting either
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft_{1}}
or

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\trianglelefteqslant}
. Implication arrows (
\Rightarrow
) represents an
implication.

Proof.

Assume we have such a write
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
, we derive a contradiction.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}
, we unfold
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}

into
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
. By
Lemma

B.1
we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
from
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft s}
. We first show
that there exists a write
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
with
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}

such that either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
or

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
hold. We split

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
into multiple cases, multiple times, as
shown in
Fig.

12
. We justify each of the
implications:

•

Any implication from something with
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft}
into either

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft_{1}\trianglelefteqslant}
or
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\trianglelefteqslant\vartriangleleft_{1}}
simply holds by
definition.

•

The implication
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
into

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}\trianglelefteqslant\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
holds since there has to
exist a write
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{j}}
and a scan
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}\vartriangleleft w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
. By

Fig.

7
, we either have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}}

or
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubset\sigma}
, where in the former case we
have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}\vartriangleleft w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}}}
,
contradicting irreflexivity of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft}
, thus we must have
the latter. Thus we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubset\sigma}}
.

•

All the implications going into
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}

holds by
Lemmas

3.1
and
RB.2
.

We now consider each of the possible final cases of

Fig.

12
:

•

If we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{wr}$}}$}}}$}}}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
, then we must
have some
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{wr}$}}$}}}$}}}w^{\prime\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
. We recursively consider
the cases of
Fig.

12
with

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
. We cannot have an infinite
recursive chain of this case, since the set of writes is finite
and thus, the only way to have such an infinite chain is by having
a cycle in
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{wr}$}}$}}}$}}}}
, which is impossible since such a cycle
implies we also have a cycle of rep events.

•

If we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
then we contradict
the result of proving
Fig.

5
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
since
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}

cannot be equal by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}}
.

Thus the only remaining cases are

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
. We case split

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and consider all possible combined
cases:

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}

happens in-between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
,
thus we contradict
Fig.

3
.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
, then from
Fig.

7
we
have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
, implying

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
happened in-between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
, thus we contradict

Fig.

3
.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
, then
Fig.

7

contradicts
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
, then
Fig.

7

contradicts
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
. ∎

Fig.

5
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i},w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i},w^{\prime}_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s^{\prime}}
with

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}}
then we cannot have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{j}\vartriangleleft w_{j}}
.

Proof.

Assume we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{j}\vartriangleleft w_{j}}
, we derive a contradiction. Let

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s^{\prime}}^{\bullet}}
,
we unfold
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i},w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
into

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i},w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}
and similarly for

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i},w^{\prime}_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s^{\prime}}
. By
Fig.

7
, we either
have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma\sqsubset\sigma^{\prime}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubset\sigma}
. If we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}}
, we
contradict the result of proving
Fig.

5
, since we
would have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
, but
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}

cannot be equal since we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}}
. If we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma\sqsubset\sigma^{\prime}}
, then we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{j}\vartriangleleft w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma\sqsubset\sigma^{\prime}}}
, contradicting the
result of proving
Fig.

5
by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{j}}
occurring
in between
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}}
. Similarly, if we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubset\sigma}
, then we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubset\sigma}}
, contradicting the
result of proving
Fig.

5
by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
occurring
in between
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
.
∎

Appendix C
Algorithm
1
Satisfies the Forwarding Signature

We here present the complete proof for Jayanti’s
single-writer/single-scanner algorithm (
Algorithm

1
) satisfying
the
\mathsf{Forwarding}
signature (
Fig.

7
).

Since this algorithm is single-scanner, we instantiate the set of
virtual scans to be equal to the set of abs scans,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\Sigma}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}S}
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}[-]^{\bullet}}
be the identity mapping,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
. We let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{A}}
correspond to the
array of the same name and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{B}}
for each virtual scan will
only map to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{B}}
. The rep events of the event signatures for

write
and
vscan
(
Fig.

6
) corresponds to the
rep events of the algorithm of the same name, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
. We
instantiate forwarding visibility to the following:

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}s}\mathrlap{\quad\triangleq}\hphantom{{}\implies{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}

I.e., a write
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
was forwarded to scan
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
if

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
read the value written by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
.

By
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}[-]^{\bullet}}
being instantiated as the identity mapping,

Fig.

7
holds, since for all
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}
,

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}
. By the algorithm being single-scanner implies
that virtual scan total order
Fig.

7
holds. The
structure of the algorithm and the instantiation of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}}

implies that
Figs.

7
and
7
hold. Additionally,
since the algorithm only writes to
\mathtt{A}
in writers and writes

\bot
to
\mathtt{B}
in scanners,
Figs.

7
and
7
.
This leaves Properties (
7
) to (
7
) to
prove.

Fig.

7
0.

If we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}s}
then

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
must be equal.

Proof.

By our instantiation we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, thus by
Fig.

3
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}={w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
. Since all writes only performs their
operations at most once, this means that the writes must be the
same, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}=w^{\prime}_{i}}
.
∎

Fig.

7
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
terminated without

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
then there exists
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}

such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}s}}
.

Proof.

Since we do not have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, it is the
case that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
could not have observed any other

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
since such
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s^{\prime}}
must have occurred before

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
by single-scanner, implying

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\sqsubset{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
, meaning we would contradict

Fig.

3
if we had

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
occurring
in between them. Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
must have observed some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
,
implying our goal
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}s}
.
∎

Fig.

7
0.

If we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}s}
then we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
without

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
.

Proof.

Since
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}s}
is instantiated as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
. For proving absence of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, assume we have it, we derive a
contradiction. By
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}s}
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, which by
Fig.

3

we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}={s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
, however that is impossible,
since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
is a rep event of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
, while

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
is a rep event of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
, thus a
contradiction.
∎

Note that we now satisfies
Figs.

7
and
7
, meaning we
satisfy the preconditions to use
Lemma

3.1
. Before we
prove
Figs.

7
,
7
and
7
, we prove some
helper lemmas.

Lemma C.1.

For any execution, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
iff we
have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\vartriangleleft{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
.

Proof.

(\mathord{\implies})

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}

trivially implies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\vartriangleleft{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
, and if we were
to have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
we contradict

Fig.

3
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}}
occurring in
between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
.

(\mathord{\impliedby})

By the fact that only scans change

\mathtt{X}
and there is a single-scanner constraint, we know that any
write to
\mathtt{X}
other than
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}}

either happens before or after the scan
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
. Since

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
has to observe something when it finishes, it
must observe
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
, since if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}

for some write event
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
, then by

Fig.

3
we either have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e\vartriangleleft{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
, which
contradicts
Fig.

3
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}

occurring in between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
; or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\vartriangleleft e}
, which by the algorithm structure

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}}
or some later write, however this
contradicts
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
. ∎

Lemma C.2.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
was
executed, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
.

Proof.

For
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
, there must exist some
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}

and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{j}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset e\trianglelefteqslant w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
. Each
of these events are populated except for
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
, since each of
the events are to the left of some
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft}
. For

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset e\trianglelefteqslant w_{j}}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
be a rep event
executed by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}
, then by
Eqs.

RB.2
and
3.1

we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\sqsubset e_{r}\trianglelefteqslant{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
. We case split

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
into either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{j}}}

or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
.

•

If we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{j}}}
then we have the chain

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}\sqsubset{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\vartriangleleft{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{j}}\sqsubset{s}_{\mathit{off}}\sqsubset{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}

which trivially implies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
.

•

If we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
then since

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
wrote, we know that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
read

\mathtt{true}
, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s^{\prime}}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
for some

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s^{\prime}}
. By single-scanner, we have either
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s\sqsubset s^{\prime}}
or

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s^{\prime}\sqsubseteq s}
, which in the former case we can form the cycle

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}\sqsubset{s^{\prime}}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}\sqsubset{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}

contradicting irreflexivity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\vartriangleleft}
, thus we must have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s^{\prime}\sqsubseteq s}
. By
Lemma

C.1
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s^{\prime}}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
, which by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s^{\prime}\sqsubseteq s}

implies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
. By
Eq.

RB.1

over
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\sqsubset e_{r}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\sqsubset{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\sqsubset{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
since if we were to have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\sqsubset e_{r}}
then we contradict

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\sqsubset e_{r}\trianglelefteqslant{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
. For the

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
goal, assume we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
, then we derive a contradiction
by having
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}\vartriangleleft{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
which
contradicts
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
. ∎

Fig.

7
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}

then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

C.2
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
was
executed. Consider if we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
, then
by
Lemma

C.1
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}

which in turn implies that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
must have executed,
however that contradicts
Fig.

3
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
occurring in between

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, thus we must have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\not\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
.

By
Eq.

RB.1
over
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\sqsubset{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
, since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\not\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}

contradicts
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\sqsubset{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
, we must have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
, which is our goal, thus we are done.
∎

Figs.

7
and
7
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}s}
and either
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\not\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}}
.

Proof.

By our instantiation we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
from

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}s}
. Assuming we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}}
(thus we
also have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
by

Lemma

3.1
), we derive a contradiction.

Let us start with the case where we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
. By the single-writer constraint
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\sqsubset{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
which
contradicts
Fig.

3
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}

occurring in between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, thus this
case is not possible. For the next case we assume

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\not\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
.

For the
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
case, by

Lemma

C.2
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
was
executed. By
Eq.

RB.1
over

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\sqsubset{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
, since we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\not\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
contradicting

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
we must have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\sqsubset{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
. By
Lemma

C.1
we thus have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
, which means that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
reads
\mathtt{true}
and thus
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
is
executed. By the single-writer constraint we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\vartriangleleft{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
which contradicts

Fig.

3
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
occurring in between

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
.
∎

Appendix D
Multi-writer Forwarding satisfies the Forwarding signature

We here give the complete proof that any history satisfying the

\mathsf{MWForwarding}
signature (
Fig.

11
) also satisfies the

\mathsf{Forwarding}
signature (
Fig.

7
). We instantiate
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\Sigma}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}[-]^{\bullet}}
of
\mathsf{Forwarding}
to be the same as the corresponding
instantiations of
\mathsf{MWForwarding}
.

Figs.

7
,
7
,
7
,
7
and
7

are shared between the signatures, therefore they trivially hold.

Fig.

7
0.

Every virtual scan satisfies

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
.

Proof.

By
Fig.

11
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xRightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{on}}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xRightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}

by
Eq.

RB.1
over
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{on}}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
, we either have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{on}}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}}
, however the latter
contradicts
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xRightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{on}}}}
, thus we must
have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
. Similarly, by

Eq.

RB.1
over
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, we either have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}
, where the latter
contradicts
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xRightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}}
, thus we
must have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
.
∎

Fig.

7
0.

If we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
must
be equal.

Proof.

By the instantiation we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
from

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}

respectively. By
Fig.

8
over

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}={f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
, implying
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f=f^{\prime}}
since each

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f}
performs
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
at most once. Thus we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}}
, which by
Fig.

3

we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}={w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
, which again since we only
have one write to
\mathtt{A}[i]
per abs write we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}=w^{\prime}_{i}}
.
∎

Fig.

7
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
terminated without

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
then there exists

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
.

Proof.

Since we do not have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, it is
the case that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
could not have observed any
other
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
since such
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\sigma^{\prime}}
must have
occurred before
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\sigma}
by virtual scanner total order

Fig.

7
, implying

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
by
Fig.

11
,
meaning we would contradict
Fig.

8
if we had

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}

occurring in between them.
By
Fig.

7
this means that whatever

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
read, it cannot have been
\bot
. By

Fig.

11
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
must have observed
some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
.
This forwarding
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f}
must have read something with

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}}
by the structure enforced by

Fig.

11
, which by
Fig.

7
must be
some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
. Thus we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, matching the instantiation of

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}}
.
∎

Fig.

7
0.

If we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
then we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
without

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
.

Proof.

Since
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
is instantiated as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, and since we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}\sqsubset{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}\sqsubset{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
,
implying
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
. For proving absence of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, assume we have it, we
derive a contradiction. By the instantiation of

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{fwd}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, which by

Fig.

3
gives us
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}={\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
,
however that is impossible, since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
is a rep event
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f}
, while
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
is a rep event of

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
, thus a contradiction.
∎

We have now satisfied
Figs.

7
and
7
, so we satisfy
the preconditions to use
Lemma

3.1
. Before we prove
Properties (
7
) to (
7
), we prove some
helper lemmas. First, we prove that if a virtual scan
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}

observes a forwarding
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f}
must have observed

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}}
both in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
,
meaning that the forward was concurrent to the scan.

Lemma D.1.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
.

Proof.

By
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, we know that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
successfully wrote to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}.\mathtt{B}[i]
, thus we know by

Fig.

8
that there exists some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
that wrote
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}.\mathtt{B}[i]
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
. Also by the execution of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f}
we
know that both
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
observed some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{on}}}
of some
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}
, i.e.,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
. To reach our goal, we prove by

Fig.

7
that we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}}
by showing
that we can neither have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma\sqsubset\sigma^{\prime}}
nor

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubset\sigma}
.

•

For
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma\sqsubset\sigma^{\prime}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
by
Eq.

RB.2

which leads to the cycle

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}\sqsubset{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}

which contradicts irreflexivity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\vartriangleleft}
(
Lemma

2.1
).

•

For
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubset\sigma}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
by
Eq.

RB.2
.
By
Fig.

8
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\vartriangleleft{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
since if we instead had

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}

occurring in between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
,
contradicting
Fig.

8
.
Similarly by
Fig.

8
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
, since consider if we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\trianglelefteqslant e_{r}}
: By
Eq.

RB.1
over

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\sqsubset{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, we either have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, which lets us construct the
following cycle contradicting irreflexivity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\vartriangleleft}

(
Lemma

2.1
)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\trianglelefteqslant e_{r}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}

or we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}\sqsubset{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
which implies

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{off}}\trianglelefteqslant{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}\sqsubset{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
, thus

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{off}}}
occurs in between

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
, contradicting

Fig.

8
.
Thus we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\vartriangleleft{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
, however this implies that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
occurs in between

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
, contradicting

Fig.

8
. ∎

Lemma D.2.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
, and for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f^{\prime}}

executed by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
and if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}

was executed, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
.

Proof.

For
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset\trianglelefteqslant\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
, there exists some

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{j}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset e\trianglelefteqslant w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
. We know all of these
events are populated except for
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
since they occur to the
left of some
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft}
. By
Lemmas

3.1
and
RB.2
,
we have every rep event of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
returns before some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
in
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\trianglelefteqslant{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
. We case
split
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
into either

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{j}}}
, or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{j}}}
then every rep event
of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
happens before
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}
since we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\trianglelefteqslant{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{j}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}

thus we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
for all

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f^{\prime}}
executed by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
then by

Lemma

D.1
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
. By
Fig.

11
we
have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
, which in turn implies

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
, since every rep event in

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
occurs before
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\trianglelefteqslant{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{a}}\sqsubset{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
. By

Eq.

RB.1
over
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\sqsubset e_{r}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, since

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}\sqsubset e_{r}}
contradicts

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\trianglelefteqslant{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}\sqsubset e_{r}\trianglelefteqslant{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}

thus we satisfy all our goals. ∎

Lemma D.3.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f^{\prime}}

executed by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f\sqsubset f^{\prime}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{x}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, then there exists some

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f^{\prime\prime}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\vartriangleleft{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\not\vartriangleleft{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\trianglelefteqslant{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}

successfully wrote to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}.\mathtt{B}[i]
.

Proof.

Since we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\vartriangleleft{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, it is not possible for any

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
to occur in the intervals of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f}
, therefore we can apply
Lemma

4.3
, thus by

Fig.

11
there exists some
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}f^{\prime\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\not\vartriangleleft{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}\trianglelefteqslant{f^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}

successfully wrote to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}.\mathtt{B}[i]
. By
Eq.

RB.1
over

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}\sqsubset{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\sqsubset{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
, since

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\not\vartriangleleft{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
contradicts

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}\sqsubset{f}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{b}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}\sqsubset{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
, which lets us construct

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}\sqsubset{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
implying
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\vartriangleleft{f^{\prime\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{\underline{b}}}
, thus
we satisfy all the goals.
∎

Fig.

0.

If

and

0.

and

D.1
