Appendix E
Algorithms
2
and
3
Satisfy the
Multi-Writer Forwarding Signature

We will show the complete proofs that
Algorithms

2
and
3
satisfy
the multi-writer forwarding signature (
Fig.

11
). We start
by repeating the proof that
Algorithm

2
satisfies the signature.

Lemma

4.6
0.

Every execution of
Algorithm

2
satisfies the
\mathsf{MWForwarding}
signature
(
Fig.

11
).

Proof.

Since this algorithm is single scanner, we can simply define the set
of virtual scans to be the same as the set of abs scans and map each
abs scan to itself, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\Sigma}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}S}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
. Each rep event directly corresponds to
their equivalent variant in
Algorithm

2
, except for

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{on}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}}
which are set as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{on}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}}

respectively.
Fig.

7
holds by each event being a
subevent of itself (
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\subseteq e}
) and
Fig.

7
holds
since the algorithm is single-scanner. Properties (
7
)
to (
11
) and (
11
) to
(
11
) holds directly by the structure of the
algorithm and
Fig.

11
can be proven by
Lemma
C.1
, the same lemma that established this property
for
Algorithm

1
, since the relative structure of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}}
is the same.
∎

We next prove that
Algorithm

3
satisfies signature. We first prove
that the phases of logical scans can only happen in sequence.

Lemma E.1.

Each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}}
that
successfully wrote to
\mathtt{X}
are ordered such that there exists
either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}^{\prime}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}^{\prime}}
,
where we have either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}
,
with the exception of the first
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}
, which observes the
initial value of
\mathtt{X}
.

Proof.

Looking at
Algorithm

3
, we see that the only operations writing
to
\mathtt{X}
are successful
\mathbf{SC}
operations, which are only events

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}}
. For either
of these events to be successful, they need to observe the same
write as the latest
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}
observed as per

Fig.

8
.

For
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}
to be successful, we must have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}\mathit{vx}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}
. For the
algorithm to execute
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}
, the write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
must have
written 1 to
x.\mathtt{phase}
, which can only be either the original
initialization of
\mathtt{X}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}}
. Similarly, for

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}}
, it can only be executed if the write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}

wrote 2 to
x.\mathtt{phase}
, thus
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
can only be
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}
,
and for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}}
, it can only be executed if the write

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
wrote 3 to
x.\mathtt{phase}
, thus
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
can only be

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}}
.
∎

We instantiate the write and forward rep events for the event
structures of
Fig.

10
with rep events of the same name.
The structure of
write
and
forward
ensures that
Properties (
7
), (
11
) and
(
11
) to (
11
) are satisfied.

We instantiate virtual scans as a logical object corresponding to
the rep events executed by
pushVS
constructing a complete
logical scan. The interval of a virtual scan
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
is
instantiated as the smallest interval containing all its rep events.
More formally, if we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}^{\prime}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}^{\prime\prime}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}\mathit{vx}^{\prime}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}\mathit{vx}^{\prime\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime\prime}}
wrote to

\mathtt{SS}
, then there exists a virtual scan
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
. Its rep
events are instantiated using the rep events of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}^{\prime\prime}}
as follows and instantiate

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s_{\mathit{ss}}}
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vr}_{i}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{on}}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{va}^{\prime}_{i}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vb}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}

We also let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{init}}\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime\prime}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}}
where

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}
was successful and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}
, however these do not need to be
in the interval of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
. We can view the events as forming
the following structure.

\begin{array}[c]{ccccccc}\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}\hfil\lx@intercol&&\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}^{\prime}}\hfil\lx@intercol&&\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}^{\prime\prime}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[-5.0pt]
\lx@intercol\hfil${\downbracefill}$\hfil\lx@intercol&&\lx@intercol\hfil${\downbracefill}$\hfil\lx@intercol&&\lx@intercol\hfil${\downbracefill}$\hfil\lx@intercol\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}\sqsubset{}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vr}_{0}\cdots\mathit{vr}_{n-1}\sqsubset\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}^{\prime}\sqsubset\mathit{va}^{\prime}_{0}\cdots\mathit{va}^{\prime}_{n-1}\sqsubset\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}^{\prime\prime}\sqsubset\mathit{vb}^{\prime\prime}_{0}\cdots\mathit{vb}^{\prime\prime}_{n-1}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{}\sqsubset\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime\prime}}\\
&\lx@intercol\hfil${\upbracefill}$\hfil\lx@intercol\\[-2.0pt]
&\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

Figs.

7
,
7
,
11
and
11

follows from the structure of virtual scans and the algorithm.

Lemma E.2.

For every virtual scan
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}

have written to
\mathtt{X}
, there exists exactly one

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the number virtual scans performed. The base case
corresponds to where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
is the very first virtual scan. We
simultaneously consider the inductive step case, where the inductive
hypothesis states that all virtual scans

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\Sigma^{\prime}}
has exactly one
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}

such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}
,
the inductive step follows that for
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
proceeding the
virtual scans of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\Sigma^{\prime}}
, there exists exactly one

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
.

For the base case, since no other virtual scans has finished prior,
we must have
x.\mathtt{sync}=\mathit{ss}.\mathtt{sync}
before
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}}

has been executed by initialization of
\mathtt{X}
and
\mathtt{SS}
. For the
inductive step, we have
x.\mathtt{sync}=\mathit{ss}.\mathtt{sync}
before

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}}
executes, since it follows from each

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\Sigma^{\prime}}
having

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}
and there only existing one

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
per
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}
, since after

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}
has executed,
x.\mathtt{sync}=\mathit{ss}.\mathtt{sync}
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
negating
\mathit{ss}.\mathtt{sync}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}

negating
x.\mathtt{sync}
, both exactly once.

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
successfully writing, we know that the
code of phase 3 (lines 31-43 of
Algorithm

3
) must have been
executed. Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}}
, we also know
that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v\underline{x}}}
returned
\mathtt{true}
, since otherwise

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
must have failed to write. If the value of

\mathit{ss}.\mathtt{sync}
read by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{ss}}}
is different from the value of

x.\mathtt{sync}
read by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{off}}}}
, then there must exist
some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}}
that negated
\mathit{ss}.\mathtt{sync}
before

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{ss}}}
(and thus before
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
), but after

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}
, which fits our goal.

Otherwise, if
\mathit{ss}.\mathtt{sync}=x.\mathtt{sync}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
will be
executed. If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
successfully wrote, then we trivially
satisfy our goal. Otherwise, by
Lemma

4.2
, there
has to exist some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}\not\vartriangleleft\mathit{v}_{\mathit{ss}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}\vartriangleleft\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
.
Since we must have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}\mathit{vx}^{\prime}\sqsubset\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}}
for
some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime\prime}}
, consider if we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}=\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime\prime}}
, then we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
, satisfying our goal.
Consider instead we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\neq\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime\prime}}
, by

Fig.

8
, we either have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime\prime}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime\prime}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}
, where if we have the former then
we must have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime\prime}\vartriangleleft\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}}
by

Lemma

E.1
, inducing the cycle

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}\vartriangleleft\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
contradicting
irreflexivity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\vartriangleleft}
, thus we must have the latter. By

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime\prime}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}
, it must be the case that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}}
correspond to some earlier executed virtual scan,
which in the base case contradicts
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
being the first
virtual scan, while in the inductive step
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}^{\prime\prime}}

corresponds to some
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\Sigma^{\prime}}
, it must be the
case that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft\mathit{v}_{\mathit{ss}}}
by
the inductive hypothesis and
Lemma

E.1
, which contradicts

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}^{\prime}\not\vartriangleleft\mathit{v}_{\mathit{ss}}}
.
∎

To efficiently reason about
pushVS
, we define the intervals of
the phases. For an execution
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
of
pushVS
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}.p_{1}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}.p_{2}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}.p_{3}}
are the
intervals of phases 1, 2, and 3. Each phase interval
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}
of

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
has a start event
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}
which is some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}
and an end event
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ee}}
which is a

\mathbf{SC}
event of
\mathtt{X}
, where start and end events need to satisfy

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{ll}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ee}}}
.
For
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{1}}
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{1}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ee}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{on}}}
with
either
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{1}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}
observing the initial value of

\mathtt{X}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{1}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}}
; for

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{2}}
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{2}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ee}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{off}}}
with

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{2}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}}
; and for
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{3}}

we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{3}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ee}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{v}_{\mathit{end}}}
with

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{3}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}}
. Each phase

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}
has an
advancement event
, which we encode with

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ae}}
, which is the rep event successfully writing
to
\mathtt{X}
which advances the logical scanner to the next phase. For
some
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{1}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ae}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{2}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ae}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{3}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ae}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
.

Lemma E.3.

For each phase
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}
, there exists a
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ae}}

such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ae}}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ae}}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ee}}}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

4.2
, there exists some successful
writing event
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
of
\mathtt{X}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ee}}}
. If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ae}}
, we are done, otherwise by

Lemma

E.1
we know that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
occur in order, so for
example in the case of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{2}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{2}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}}
, by

Fig.

8
we must have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\vartriangleleft e_{r}}
,
since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}}
contradicts

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}}
. Thus by

Lemma

E.1
, there exists some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{2}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ae}}}
in between

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
, which satisfies

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ee}}}
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft e_{r}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{ee}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}}
since

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}}
contradicts

Fig.

8
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{off}}}
occurring in
between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{2}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{se}}}
. The other
phases follow by similar arguments.
∎

Lemma E.4.

For any terminated
pushVS
execution
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
, there
exists some
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
such that for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}
being the first
rep event of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
being the last, then and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}}
.

Proof.

Consider whether
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{3}}
was executed or not by
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
. If

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{3}}
was executed, then by
Lemma

E.3
we satisfy
our goal. Otherwise, if it was not executed, this means either

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{1}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{2}}
were executed, i.e., there is some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
setting
x.\mathtt{phase}
, thus
there is another chance for
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{3}}
to run. If again it was not
run, we are either in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{1}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}p_{2}}
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}/{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{on}}}
, by
Lemma

E.3
the
execution of virtual scans must have progressed, and by

Lemma

E.1
there must be some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}

between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}/{\sigma}_{\mathit{on}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}/{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{on}}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}
may
be equal to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}
. Thus
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}

satisfies our goal.
∎

Lemma E.5.

For any terminated
pushVS
executions
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}^{\prime}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}\sqsubset\mathit{v}^{\prime}}
, there exists some

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}
such that for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}
being the
first rep event of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

E.4
over
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}^{\prime}}
, we
have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}^{\prime}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e^{\prime}_{r}}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e^{\prime}_{r}}
are the last events of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{v}^{\prime}}

respectively. By
Fig.

8
, we must have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}
, since if we instead had

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\trianglelefteqslant{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
, we contradict

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}^{\prime}}}
by having

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\trianglelefteqslant{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\trianglelefteqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}^{\prime}}}
.
∎

Fig.

7
0.

For any abs scan
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
.

Proof.

With
Lemma

E.5
, we can show for
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
that there
exists some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}
in between

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{sv_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{sv_{2}}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}
is the
first rep event of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\mathit{sv_{1}}}
. For
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}
,
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{init}}}
since some new
logical scan must have started after
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}}
occurring after it, and by the instantiation
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}[-]^{\bullet}}
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s_{\mathit{ss}}}
, thus for all

i
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{init}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s_{\mathit{ss}}}
.
Our goal is to establish

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{start}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}.\mathsf{start}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}.\mathsf{end}\leq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{end}
.
We show this by showing that we neither have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{start}>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}.\mathsf{start}
nor

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}.\mathsf{end}>{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{end}
.

•

Assume we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{start}>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}.\mathsf{start}
, we derive a
contradiction. By definition of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{init}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{init}}}.\mathsf{end}<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}.\mathsf{start}
,
which implies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{init}}}.\mathsf{end}<{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{start}
and
thus
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
. This in turn implies

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}\sqsubset\mathit{vx}}
since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
,
which contradicts
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\not\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathit{vx}}}
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{init}}\sqsubset\mathit{vx}}
.

•

Assume we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}.\mathsf{end}>{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{end}
,
we derive a contradiction. By definition of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}.\mathsf{end}<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}.\mathsf{start}
, which
implies
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{end}<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}.\mathsf{start}
and thus

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
. This in turn implies

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}s_{\mathit{ss}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}s_{\mathit{ss}}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
, which
contradict irreflexivity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\vartriangleleft}
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}s_{\mathit{ss}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s_{\mathit{ss}}}
. ∎

Fig.

11
0.

For any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
reading
\mathtt{X}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}e_{r}}

iff we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\vartriangleleft e_{r}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\not\vartriangleleft e_{r}}
.

Proof.

(\mathord{\implies})

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
trivially
implies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\vartriangleleft{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
, and if we were to have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\vartriangleleft{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
we contradict
Fig.

3

since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}}
would happen in-between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
.

(\mathord{\impliedby})

By the fact that only virtual scans
change
\mathtt{X}
and by
Lemma

E.1
forcing these changes to
be of an order of form

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s}_{\mathit{off}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s^{\prime}}_{\mathit{on}}\dots}\
, we
know that any write to
\mathtt{X}
other than
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}}
either happens before
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
or after

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{off}}}
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathit{x}}
has to observe something when
it finishes, it must observe
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s}_{\mathit{on}}}
, since any other
observation leads to a contradiction. ∎

Fig.

7
0.

For two distinct virtual scans
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}
, we either
have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma\sqsubset\sigma^{\prime}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubset\sigma}
.

Proof.

By the instantiation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
and
Lemma

E.1
, we
have that each virtual scan must be separated by some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
, i.e., we either have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{init}}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{init}}}
. By
Lemma

E.2
,
we know that for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
there is exactly one

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}}
occurring before
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}}
, thus we
have either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{init}}}

or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{init}}}
. If we
have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{init}}}
then
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{init}}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
, and dually for

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{\underline{ss}}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathit{end}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}_{\mathit{init}}}
, thus we
either have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{r}_{i}}}
for each
i
, which matches
the defined bounds of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
, i.e., we either have

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma\sqsubset\sigma^{\prime}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubset\sigma}
, which is our goal.
∎

Appendix F
Algorithm
4
Satisfies the Snapshot Signature

We show that
Algorithm

4
satisfies the
\mathsf{Snapshot}
signatures
(
Fig.

5
). We employ virtual scans as a simplification
mechanism that allows us to elide
write
views from
consideration when establishing
\mathsf{Snapshot}
. A virtual scan will
denote a scan that detected no change in the array, i.e., it returned
at line
18
. Formally, a virtual scan
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}

consists of rep events
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}

for each
i
, where both
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
observe the same write (i.e., no change
detected), and every
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
occurs before any

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
:

\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma},i.

\exists{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}.\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}

(
5
revisited)

\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma},i,j.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}

(
5
revisited)

To each abs scan, we can associate a virtual scan as follows. If the
abs scan terminated because it detected no change, than it immediately
is a virtual scan. If the abs scan terminated by returning a view from
a
write
, i.e., returned at line
13
, then that
view itself is an abs scan which can, recursively, be associated with
a virtual scan. We formally define this by
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}[-]}^{\bullet}}
as
follows:

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}\triangleq\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}&\text{where}\quad\exists k.\ \forall i.\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}={s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}={s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}\\
\hline\cr\\[-10.00002pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}^{\bullet}}&\text{where}\quad\begin{aligned} \exists&{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime\prime}_{i}},j,k.\ j<k\land{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime\prime}_{i}\sqsubset w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset w_{i}}\land{}\\
&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}\end{aligned}\end{cases}

We instantiate
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathbb{W}_{i}}
to be the set of all writes

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}\in{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}W_{i}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
is defined; these
are the writes that executed their effect. We also instantiate the
visibility relations
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\prec}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}}
as follows, using
the helper relation
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}}
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}

\displaystyle\mathrlap{\quad\triangleq}\hphantom{{}\implies{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}

\displaystyle\mathrlap{\quad\triangleq}\hphantom{{}\implies{}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\prec}

\displaystyle\mathrlap{\quad\triangleq}\hphantom{{}\implies{}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}}

In English, the scan
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
reads from, and also observes,

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
iff there is an appropriate rep event in the virtual scan

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}
that reads from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
at the level of rep
events.

Lemma F.1.

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}[-]}^{\bullet}}
is a well-founded recursive definition.

Proof.

The first case of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}[-]}^{\bullet}}
is immediately well-founded.
For the second case it is well-founded because the ending time of
the scan
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}
on the right is smaller than the ending
time of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
on the left, and the ending times are bounded from
below by
0
. To see that the ending time of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}
is
below that of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
, suppose otherwise. Then it must be

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
, because from the code of
write

we have that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}\sqsubset{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
. In particular,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
for every
k
. But,
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
is observed by some rep read in
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
,
we also have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
for some
k
.
Thus, we have an event
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
that terminated
before the event
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
that it observes, which
contradicts
Eq.

V.1
.
∎

Lemma F.2.

For each scan
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
.

Proof.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
corresponds to the first case, then
this is trivial, since
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}
consists of the selected rep
events of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}={s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}={s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
.
Otherwise,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}})^{\bullet}}
corresponds
to the second case for some write view
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}
. By
recursion on the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}[-]^{\bullet}}
it must be

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}})^{\bullet}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}
,
so it suffices to show
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
.
By definition, if we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{start}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}.\mathsf{start}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}.\mathsf{end}\leq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{end}
then we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
. During the scan, at least three
different writes must have been observed:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
where we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime\prime}_{i}\sqsubset w^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubset w_{i}}
by the algorithm being single-writer.
If we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
we
contradict
Fig.

3
for

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
since we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
, thus
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\not\sqsubset{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
, which by
definition gives us

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}.\mathsf{start}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}.\mathsf{end}
.
Similarly,
if we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
we
contradict
Fig.

3
for

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
since we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\sqsubset{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
, thus
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}\not\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
, which by
definition gives us

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}.\mathsf{start}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}.\mathsf{end}
.
Together, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{start}<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}.\mathsf{start}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}.\mathsf{end}<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}.\mathsf{start}\ ,

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}.\mathsf{end}<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}.\mathsf{start}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{s_{k}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}.\mathsf{end}<{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{end}\ ,

which gives us

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{start}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}.\mathsf{start}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}.\mathsf{end}\leq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{end}
, i.e.,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{s}}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
.
∎

Eq.

V.1
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\prec^{+}e^{\prime}}
then we cannot have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}\sqsubseteq e}
.

Proof.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\prec^{+}e^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}\sqsubseteq e}
then

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\prec^{+}e^{\prime}}
can only hold if
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}e^{\prime}}
, i.e., we
have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
. We have that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}\sqsubseteq e}
can only be
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}\sqsubset e}
, since
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}
cannot be equal events by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}
being a write and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}
being a scan. But then, by

Eqs.

RB.2
and
F.2
, from
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}\sqsubset e}
we
derive
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}\sqsubset{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
, which contradicts

Eq.

V.1
for registers.
∎

Fig.

5
0.

For every terminated scan
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}
and index
i
, there exists some
write
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}.\mathsf{in}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}.\mathsf{out}[i]
.

Fig.

5
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
then we must have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}=w^{\prime}_{i}}
.

Proof.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
are both observed by some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
, i.e., the same read. Thus, by

Fig.

3
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}={w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
, and since
each write executes
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
only once by
Property
(ES.2)
, it must be
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}=w^{\prime}_{i}}
.
∎

Proof.

The structure of the algorithm ensures that at every return of

scan
, writes of every memory cell are observed and returned.
∎

Fig.

5
0.

For two distinct writes
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
, we have either

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}}
or
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft w_{i}}
.

Proof.

Holds by the assumption that the algorithm is single-writer.
∎

Fig.

5
0.

Every terminated write is effectful, i.e.,

\mathcal{T}({\color[rgb]{1,0,1}W_{i}})\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathbb{W}_{i}}
.

Proof.

Since each terminated write must have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
defined,
which is how we define the set
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathbb{W}_{i}}
, thus every
terminated write must be in some
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathbb{W}_{i}}
.
∎

Lemma F.3.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft s}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}
.

Proof.

This proof follows similarly to the proof of
Lemma

B.1
.
By viewing
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft s}
as a chain of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft_{1}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft_{1}\dots\vartriangleleft_{1}s}
. By induction on the length of the
chain. In our base case we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft_{1}s}
, which splits
into cases
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\sqsubset s}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\prec s}
, where the
latter can only be
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
. For
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\sqsubset s}
, by

Eqs.

RB.2
and
F.2
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\sqsubset{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}
. For
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}}
directly from the definition of

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}}
. The inductive step is similar.
∎

Lemma F.4.

Choose some
j
. For events

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e},{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}\in\bigcup_{i}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}W_{i}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\Sigma}
where

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}
is either a write
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
is defined or a virtual scan

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}
, if
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\vartriangleleft e^{\prime}}
holds then there
exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e^{\prime}_{r}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\vartriangleleft e^{\prime}_{r}}
,
where if
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}\in{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}W_{i}}
then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
and if

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\Sigma}
then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
, and dually for
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e^{\prime}_{r}}
.

Proof.

This proof follows similarly to the proof of

Lemma

3.1
. We can view
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\vartriangleleft e^{\prime}}
as a chain of

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft_{1}}
relations connecting
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}
; that
is,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\vartriangleleft_{1}\cdots\vartriangleleft_{1}e^{\prime}}
, where by definition

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft_{1}}=({\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\sqsubset}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\prec})=({\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\sqsubset}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}})
. It is easy to see that each abs event in this chain
is populated with a rep event. Indeed, if an abs event’s relation

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft_{1}}
to its successor is realized by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\sqsubset}
, then the
event is terminated and all of its rep events are executed (and by
the structure of the algorithm, each abs event must have at least
one rep event). Alternatively, if the abs event’s relation

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\vartriangleleft_{1}}
to its successor is realized by
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\prec}
, then the
event must be populated as per the definitions of reads-from. This
leaves out
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}
, which has no successor, but
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}
is
populated by either being a write, thus having
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}

defined by assumption, or being a virtual scan, which are first
defined when a scan returns.

Now the proof is by induction on the length of the above chain. The
base case is when
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\vartriangleleft_{1}e^{\prime}}
. If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\sqsubset e^{\prime}}
, the proof
is by
Eq.

RB.2
giving us
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}\sqsubset e^{\prime}_{r}}
where if

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}\in{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}W_{i}}
then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
and if

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\Sigma}
then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e_{r}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
, and dually for
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}e^{\prime}_{r}}
. For
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}e^{\prime}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,1}e^{\prime}}
with

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}}
. By
Section

5
we
construct

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
which
gives us our goal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
. The
inductive step is similar, with the addition that we need
transitivity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\vartriangleleft}
to join events.
∎

Fig.

5
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
then there does not exist a write

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft s}
holds.

Proof.

Given
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
, we assume there exists
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i}}
such
that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft s}
, we derive a contradiction. By
assumption, we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
for

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}
. By
Lemma

F.3
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft s}
, we can derive

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
, and then by

Lemma

F.4
, also

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
. By the
definition of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}}
and
Section

5
,

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
implies

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}},{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
. But then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
occurrs between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
, contradicting
Fig.

3
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{i}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
.
∎

Fig.

5
0.

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i},w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i},w^{\prime}_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s^{\prime}}

with
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}}
then we cannot have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{j}\vartriangleleft w_{j}}
.

Proof.

Given
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i},w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i},w^{\prime}_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s^{\prime}}
with

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i}\vartriangleleft w^{\prime}_{i}}
, we show that also having
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{j}\vartriangleleft w_{j}}

lead to a contradiction. We unfold the assumptions

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i},w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i},w^{\prime}_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}s^{\prime}}
into

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{i},w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{i},w^{\prime}_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}}
, where

{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s}^{\bullet}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}s^{\prime}}^{\bullet}}
.
By the definition of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}}
and
Section

5
,
from
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma}}
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{j}},{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
, and similarly
for
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}w^{\prime}_{j}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\pagecolor{lightergray}$\displaystyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{rf}$}}$}}}$}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}\sigma^{\prime}}}
. By
Section

5
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{j}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
. By
Eq.

RB.1
, it
is either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{j}}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{i}}\sqsubset{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{i}}}
. In the first case (the
second is symmetric) we can construct

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}_{j}}\sqsubset{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
, meaning that we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\vartriangleleft{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
contradicting

Fig.

3
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}}
occurring between

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}{w^{\prime}_{j}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{a}}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{{$\xrightarrow{{\raisebox{-2.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rf}}$}}}$}}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathit{b}_{j}}}
.
∎
